        Idea:
Add the full resident Discord operator tool layer discussed with the user. Extend the resident Megaplan profile so the Discord agent can safely use the existing Megaplan store/CLI surfaces for: search_messages, search_epics, search_plans, search_code_artifacts, list_codebases, register_codebase/add_repo, reconcile_epic, reconcile_plan_storage, list_repos, read_plan_artifact, write_plan_artifact, export_epic_bundle, archive_cloud_logs, and open/start Megaplan runs where appropriate. Preserve the constrained security model: broad write/destructive/cloud/repo mutation actions require admin authorization and exact confirmation where comparable to cloud start; read/search tools should respect existing allowlists. Use existing Store methods and DB/FileStore implementations where possible instead of inventing parallel persistence. Include a clear repo/cloud approach: registered repos/codebases should be durable DB/FileStore records; if a repo needed for cloud work is missing from the cloud machine, cloud bootstrap/deploy should clone from the registered repo URL/branch into the configured workspace, reusing the existing cloud entrypoint clone-if-missing behavior where possible and adding only the minimal missing resident-facing tool/config glue. Add focused tests for the new resident tool registrations, search behavior, repo/codebase registration/listing, plan artifact read/write, export invocation, reconciliation summaries, and cloud/repo safety confirmations. Do not expose arbitrary shell execution or unrestricted filesystem editing through Discord.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
Add the full resident Discord operator tool layer discussed with the user. Extend the resident Megaplan profile so the Discord agent can safely use the existing Megaplan store/CLI surfaces for: search_messages, search_epics, search_plans, search_code_artifacts, list_codebases, register_codebase/add_repo, reconcile_epic, reconcile_plan_storage, list_repos, read_plan_artifact, write_plan_artifact, export_epic_bundle, archive_cloud_logs, and open/start Megaplan runs where appropriate. Preserve the constrained security model: broad write/destructive/cloud/repo mutation actions require admin authorization and exact confirmation where comparable to cloud start; read/search tools should respect existing allowlists. Use existing Store methods and DB/FileStore implementations where possible instead of inventing parallel persistence. Include a clear repo/cloud approach: registered repos/codebases should be durable DB/FileStore records; if a repo needed for cloud work is missing from the cloud machine, cloud bootstrap/deploy should clone from the registered repo URL/branch into the configured workspace, reusing the existing cloud entrypoint clone-if-missing behavior where possible and adding only the minimal missing resident-facing tool/config glue. Add focused tests for the new resident tool registrations, search behavior, repo/codebase registration/listing, plan artifact read/write, export invocation, reconciliation summaries, and cloud/repo safety confirmations. Do not expose arbitrary shell execution or unrestricted filesystem editing through Discord.

    "Keep the Discord-facing surface constrained: no shell, exec, arbitrary clone, arbitrary remote command, or unrestricted filesystem write tools.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state["current_state"] = state_executed` assignment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: stop-signal persistence is internally inconsistent. step 5 adds `stop_signal` to `execution_doc.json` task updates, but only adds `stance` to `finalize.json` tasks; step 11 then says `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` reads latest `finalize.json` for `stance.stop_signal.requested`. in the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11's pseudocode calls `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` from inside `_resolve_review_outcome()`, but the current `_resolve_review_outcome()` signature has no `plan_dir` parameter and both call sites pass only review counts, robustness, state, issues, and criteria (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:148-190`, `332-337`, `483-488`). the plan needs to specify adding `plan_dir` or pass precomputed stop-signal data into the resolver; otherwise the helper has no path to read finalize or directors-notes data. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the creative-light transition target is not technically executable as written. `workflow[state_gated]` has `transition("finalize", state_finalized)` and `handle_finalize()` requires `state_gated`, but the proposed creative-light override is `state_planned: [transition("finalize", state_gated)]`; no current handler applies that `next_state` before running `finalize`. `workflow_transition()` is only used for tiebreaker and revise/gate paths, while `_finish_step()` uses `workflow_next()` for display only, so this transition would produce an invalid next step from a planned state. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] completeness: handle_init() and override flows also emit next_step but aren't listed for next_step_runtime enrichment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] completeness: handle_execute() at handlers.py:1152-1175 can clear next_step to none but leave stale next_step_runtime. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] completeness: next_step_runtime fires on previous step completion, not literally when the next phase starts. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] completeness: test_command config override unreachable via normal init/cli flow (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

- [DEBT] correctness: resolve_phase_runtime called on non-phase next_step values would fail. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] correctness: non-phase next_step values from workflow_next() not filtered. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

          if data is None:\n+                continue\n+            files.append({\n+                \"path\": f\"plan_artifacts/{plan.id}/{ref.name}\",\n+                \"kind\": \"plan_artifact\",\n+                \"plan_id\": plan.id,\n+                \"artifact_name\": ref.name,\n+                \"bytes\": data,\n+                \"size_bytes\": len(data),\n+                \"sha256\": hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest(),\n+            })\n+    manifest = {\n+        \"format\": \"megaplan-epic-export-v1\",\n+        \"epic_id\": epic_id,\n+        \"file_count\": len(files),\n+        \"files\": [{key: value for key, value in file.items() if key != \"bytes\"} for file in sorted(files, key=lambda item: item[\"path\"])],\n+        \"warnings\": [],\n+        \"errors\": [],\n+    }\n+    return {\"epic_id\": epic_id, \"files\": sorted(files, key=lambda item: item[\"path\"]), \"manifest\": manifest, \"warnings\": [], \"errors\": []}\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state["current_state"] = state_executed` assignment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: stop-signal persistence is internally inconsistent. step 5 adds `stop_signal` to `execution_doc.json` task updates, but only adds `stance` to `finalize.json` tasks; step 11 then says `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` reads latest `finalize.json` for `stance.stop_signal.requested`. in the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11's pseudocode calls `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` from inside `_resolve_review_outcome()`, but the current `_resolve_review_outcome()` signature has no `plan_dir` parameter and both call sites pass only review counts, robustness, state, issues, and criteria (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:148-190`, `332-337`, `483-488`). the plan needs to specify adding `plan_dir` or pass precomputed stop-signal data into the resolver; otherwise the helper has no path to read finalize or directors-notes data. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/template.py\n@@\n-from pathlib import Path\n+from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath\n@@\n-from megaplan.cloud.spec import CloudSpec, ToolchainSpec\n+from megaplan.cloud.spec import CloudSpec, RepoSpec, ToolchainSpec\n@@\n     \"AUTO_PLAN_NAME\",\n     \"AGENT_ROUTING_BLOCK\",\n     \"CLAUDE_AUTH_BLOCK\",\n+    \"ENSURE_REPO_BLOCK\",\n     \"RUNNER_LAUNCH_BLOCK\",\n )\n@@\n def _quoted(script: str) -> str:\n     return shlex.quote(script.strip())\n+\n+\n+def render_ensure_repo_command(repo: RepoSpec) -> str:\n+    \"\"\"Render the fixed clone-if-missing command used by cloud entrypoints.\"\"\"\n+    workspace = PurePosixPath(repo.workspace)\n+    parent = str(workspace.parent)\n+    git_dir = str(workspace / \".git\")\n+    return \" && \".join(\n+        [\n+            f\"mkdir -p {shlex.quote(parent)}\",\n+            (\n+                f\"if [ ! -d {shlex.quote(git_dir)} ]; then \"\n+                f\"git clone --branch {shlex.quote(repo.branch)} \"\n+                f\"{shlex.quote(repo.url)} {shlex.quote(repo.workspace)}; \"\n+                \"else true; fi\"\n+            ),\n+        ]\n+    )\n@@\n         \"AGENT_ROUTING_BLOCK\": _agent_routing_block(spec),\n         \"CLAUDE_AUTH_BLOCK\": _claude_auth_block(),\n+        \"ENSURE_REPO_BLOCK\": render_ensure_repo_command(spec.repo),\n         \"RUNNER_LAUNCH_BLOCK\": _runner_block(spec),\n     }\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state["current_state"] = state_executed` assignment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: stop-signal persistence is internally inconsistent. step 5 adds `stop_signal` to `execution_doc.json` task updates, but only adds `stance` to `finalize.json` tasks; step 11 then says `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` reads latest `finalize.json` for `stance.stop_signal.requested`. in the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state["current_state"] = state_executed` assignment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state["current_state"] = state_executed` assignment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`. root.add_subparsers(dest=\"command\", required=True)\n+    build_cloud_parser(subparsers)\n+    return root\n+\n+\n+def spec():\n+    return CloudSpec(\n+        provider=\"railway\",\n+        repo=RepoSpec(\n+            url=\"https://github.com/example/original.git\",\n+            branch=\"main\",\n+            workspace=\"/workspace/app\",\n+        ),\n+        agents={},\n+        codex=CodexSpec(),\n+        mode=\"idle\",\n+        megaplan=MegaplanSpec(),\n+        resources=ResourcesSpec(),\n+        secrets=[],\n+        railway=RailwaySpec(service=\"agent\"),\n+        toolchains=[],\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def run_cli(tmp_root, argv, events):\n+    provider = StubProvider(events)\n+    old_get_provider = cloud_cli.get_provider\n+    old_load_spec = cloud_cli.load_spec\n+    old_marker_dir = cloud_cli._marker_dir\n+\n+    def marker_dir(_cloud_yaml):\n+        path = tmp_root / \"markers\"\n+        path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+        return path\n+\n+    try:\n+        cloud_cli.get_provider = lambda provider_name, cloud_spec: provider\n+        cloud_cli.load_spec = lambda path: spec()\n+        cloud_cli._marker_dir = marker_dir\n+        assert run_cloud_cli(tmp_root, parser().parse_args(argv)) == 0\n+    finally:\n+        cloud_cli.get_provider = old_get_provider\n+        cloud_cli.load_spec = old_load_spec\n+        cloud_cli._marker_dir = old_marker_dir\n+\n+\n+def assert_checkout_precedes_upload(events, expected_command):\n+    first_ssh = next(i for i, event in enumerate(events) if event[0] == \"ssh\")\n+    first_upload = next(i for i, event in enumerate(events) if event[0] == \"upload\")\n+    assert first_ssh < first_upload, events\n+    assert events[first_ssh] == (\"ssh\", expected_command, False), events\n+    assert \"[ ! -d /workspace/megaplan/.git ]\" in expected_command\n+    assert \"git clone --branch feature/resident https://github.com/openai/megaplan.git /workspace/megaplan\" in expected_command\n+\n+\n+def main():\n+    with TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n+        root = Path(tmp)\n+        cloud_yaml = root / \"cloud.yaml\"\n+        cloud_yaml.write_text(\"provider: railway\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+        idea = root / \"idea.txt\"\n+        idea.write_text(\"resident tool layer\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+        ideas = root / \"ideas\"\n+        ideas.mkdir()\n+        sprint_idea = ideas / \"sprint.md\"\n+        sprint_idea.write_text(\"sprint\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+        chain = root / \"chain.yaml\"\n+        chain.write_text(\n+            \"milestones:\\n\"\n+            \"  - label: sprint\\n\"\n+            \"    idea: /workspace/megaplan/ideas/sprint.md\\n\",\n+            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n+        )\n+        repo_args = [\n+            \"--repo-url\",\n+ ]\n+        expected = _ensure_repo_command(\n+            apply_repo_overrides(\n+                spec(),\n+                repo_url=\"https://github.com/openai/megaplan.git\",\n+                repo_branch=\"feature/resident\",\n+                repo_workspace=\"/workspace/megaplan\",\n+            )\n+        )\n+\n+        bootstrap_events = []\n+        run_cli(root, [\"cloud\", \"bootstrap\", str(idea), \"--cloud-yaml\", str(cloud_yaml), *repo_args], bootstrap_events)\n+        assert_checkout_precedes_upload(bootstrap_events, expected)\n+        assert (\"upload\", str(idea.resolve()), \"/workspace/megaplan/idea.txt\") in bootstrap_events, bootstrap_events\n+\n+        chain_events = []\n+        run_cli(\n+            root,\n+            [\"cloud\", \"chain\", str(chain), \"--idea-dir\", str(root), \"--cloud-yaml\", str(cloud_yaml), *repo_args],\n+            chain_events,\n+        )\n+        assert_checkout_precedes_upload(chain_events, expected)\n+ print(\"repo-aware cloud verification passed\")\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    main()\n*** End Patch\n"
